A timeline, because supersession has to be **drawn** or it will be misremembered; and a comparison, because the episode on the timeline is only worth teaching for the argument in the comparison.

### 1. The operative map, stated with its edition number and its confirmation date

✅ **The operative Indian standard is IS 1893 (Part 1): 2016, and it divides the country into four seismic zones, II, III, IV and V.** ❗ That standard was **confirmed as the standard in force by a Gazette notification of 3 March 2026**, which withdrew the revision described in §3 and restored it with immediate effect.

| ✅ Zone | ✅ Risk and MSK intensity descriptor | ✅ Representative areas |
|---|---|---|
| **Zone V** | **Very high damage risk; MSK IX and above** | Parts of the Himalayan and north-eastern belt, the **Rann of Kachchh** and the **Andaman and Nicobar Islands** |
| **Zone IV** | **High damage risk; MSK VIII** | **Delhi** and extensive Himalayan, foothill and adjoining plain sectors |
| **Zone III** | **Moderate damage risk; MSK VII** | Large parts of peninsular and coastal India |
| **Zone II** | **Low damage risk; MSK VI or lower** | Remaining comparatively stable areas |

⚠️ ✅ The Advanced owner's compact Prelims form is worth holding alongside it: **Zone V (highest): north-east India, the Himalaya across Jammu and Kashmir, Himachal Pradesh and Uttarakhand, Kutch, and the Andaman and Nicobar Islands; Delhi-NCR is Zone IV; the interior Peninsula is Zone II.** ✅ The basis of the zonation is *"expected intensity/damage and design ground motion."*

### 2. Why there is no Zone I — a supersession, taught as a supersession

❗ ✅ The Advanced owner lists it as one of three standing traps: ❌ *"Highest Indian seismic zone is Zone I."* → ⚠️ *"The BIS map runs Zone II-V; Zone V is the HIGHEST-risk (there is no Zone I in the current map)."*

⚠️ **But "there is no Zone I" is only half a teaching point. The other half is why.** An earlier Indian zonation ran **I to V**, and the older literature — including a map still reproduced in standard textbooks — uses that scheme, with its hazard classes expressed in **MSK intensity**: **very high at MSK IX, high at VIII, moderate at VII, low at VI and very low at V.** ❗ **The lowest class was merged upward** when the scheme was revised, because a zone requiring effectively no seismic design action is not a useful instruction to an engineer and because the evidence did not support treating any substantial part of the country as requiring none.

❗ **Two consequences for a candidate, and both are examinable.** ⚠️ **One**, when an older map or table showing Zones I to V is encountered, it is a **superseded earlier standard**, and the correct response is to say so rather than to renumber it silently or to assume the source is wrong. ⚠️ **Two**, the absence of Zone I is not an arbitrary numbering quirk: it is the fossil of a deliberate decision that **the minimum design requirement in India is not zero**. ❗ That is why "there is no Zone I" is a **trap** rather than a piece of trivia — the examiner is testing whether the candidate knows a map changed.

⚠️ **A second normalisation attaches to the same map.** ✅ The textbook hazard map's **legend is in MSK intensity** while the accompanying text refers to the **Modified Mercalli** scale. ❗ Both are intensity scales and they are broadly comparable, but they are not identical and should not be used interchangeably in a precise statement; **MSK is the scale used in the Indian zonation descriptors**, and this edition uses MSK where the owner does.

### 3. The 2025-26 episode — a dated supersession and what it actually teaches

📰 **The sequence, with both dates.**

| 📰 Date | Event |
|---|---|
| **6 November 2025** | The **Bureau of Indian Standards** issued a **Gazette notification** introducing revised earthquake hazard zoning under **IS 1893 (Part 1): 2025**, intended to replace the 2016 standard. It moved to an **updated probabilistic seismic hazard assessment framework** with new hazard parameters; under it, **more than 60 per cent of India's landmass** would fall in moderate-to-high risk zones, and a **new highest-risk Zone VI** was introduced, covering **parts of Kashmir, the North-East and the Kutch region of Gujarat** |
| mid-2025 | The draft was circulated among stakeholders. The **National Dam Safety Authority** raised scientific objections, reporting that the proposed hazard values were *"much higher than what we were reading from our site investigations"* |
| **February 2026** | The **Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs** wrote to the **Cabinet Secretariat** flagging *"certain technical and financial concerns"* and *"materially significant financial implications for infrastructure and construction projects"*, particularly **metro rail systems** |
| **3 March 2026** | The **Bureau of Indian Standards** issued a **fresh Gazette notification withdrawing** the November 2025 notification, **restoring IS 1893 (Part 1): 2016 with immediate effect** |

📰 **The quantified detail reported alongside the withdrawal**, which is what makes the episode teachable rather than merely newsworthy: under the withdrawn framework about **45 per cent** of the landmass would have seen **no zoning change** — largely Zone II and III across southern and central India; about **42 per cent** would have seen a **one-zone increase** — parts of Gujarat, northern India and the North-East — raising building construction costs by **10 to 20 per cent**; and about **6 per cent** would have seen a **two-zone increase** — parts of the Himalayan belt and Gujarat — raising costs by **over 30 per cent**. Dam infrastructure costs would have risen **10 to 20 per cent** in several regions and **up to 50 per cent** in the highest-risk zones. Experts noted that peak ground acceleration values in the revised model were allowed to rise close to **1 g**, against global practice nearer **0.4 g**. And roughly **80 per cent** of Indian housing is **"non-engineered"** self-built construction.

❗ **The frozen position of this edition, stated without ambiguity.**

1. ✅ **The operative standard is IS 1893 (Part 1): 2016, with four zones II to V.**
2. ❗ **Zone VI is not the current map.** It was introduced on **6 November 2025** and **withdrawn on 3 March 2026**. It is taught here **only as a withdrawn proposal, dated on both ends**.
3. ❗ **Evidentiary limit, disclosed rather than concealed.** The two Gazette notifications were **not obtained as primary documents** for this edition. The account rests on **first-order reporting that quotes both notifications by date**, corroborated across **multiple independent outlets** and a dated daily-news index entry. ⚠️ It is recorded at that evidentiary level and **not upgraded to a first-party citation it does not have**.
4. ⚠️ **It also explains a claim in circulation.** Material circulating in late 2025 and afterwards asserting an Indian "Zone VI" refers to a notification that was **live for under four months**. ❗ Such a claim is **time-bounded and now superseded** — which is itself a transferable lesson in source criticism: **a standard has an edition number and a date, and a statement about it is only as current as the date attached to it.**

⚠️ **What the episode teaches, and this is why it is in the lesson at all.** ❗ **A seismic zone is a design decision, not a scientific prediction.** Shifting from a largely deterministic to a probabilistic hazard basis changed **design obligations, costs and politics before it changed any rock**. The tectonics of Kashmir, the North-East and Kutch were identical on 5 November 2025, on 1 January 2026 and on 4 March 2026; what changed, twice, was **the administrative instrument that converts hazard into an obligation**.

⚠️ **The deterministic-against-probabilistic argument, stated properly.** A deterministic-leaning basis asks what the worst credible event at a site is and designs for it; a probabilistic assessment integrates **all** sources, weighted by their rates of occurrence, into a hazard curve, from which an exceedance level is **chosen**. ❗ The second is more complete and more transparent about its assumptions — and it is **more assumption-laden**, because it requires an explicit source model, a recurrence model and an attenuation model, each of which is contestable. ⚠️ **That is precisely the shape of the objection recorded in the episode**: a technical body reported that the modelled values exceeded what its own site investigations indicated. ❗ **The dispute was about the model, not about the geology.**

### 4. Macro-zonation and microzonation — refinement, not replacement

⚠️ **The relation is the examinable point, and it is routinely got wrong.** A macro-zonation map is a **national design generalisation** drawn at small scale; it cannot represent variation within a city. ❗ **Microzonation does not replace it and does not create new national zones**; it **refines** the hazard characterisation within a zone, at large scale, using site conditions.

📰 ✅ **The definition and the programme, first-party.** Seismic hazard microzonation is *"a process of classifying a region into zones of relatively similar exposure to various earthquake-related effects"*, delivering *"user-friendly, GIS-based and site-specific hazard and risk related information products to enable appropriate planning of pre- and post-disaster management strategies."* 📰 ✅ **Microzonation of the Delhi region has been completed on a 1:10,000 scale**; the Centre *"played a key role in various studies relating to the seismic microzonation of other cities, such as **Jabalpur and Guwahati**"*; and studies of **Chennai, Coimbatore, Bhubaneshwar and Mangalore are in progress**.

⚠️ **Read that against Lesson 13 and the two lessons lock together.** The ground effects of Lesson 13 — amplification, liquefaction, resonance — are **site** phenomena operating over hundreds of metres; a national zone boundary is drawn over hundreds of kilometres. ❗ **Microzonation is therefore the scale at which Lesson 13's physics becomes a planning instrument**, and the **1:10,000 scale** is the number that makes that concrete: at that scale a map distinguishes individual neighbourhoods, which is exactly the resolution at which amplification and liquefaction vary.

⚠️ **One further distinction worth a clause.** ✅ **Delhi is in Zone IV** on the national map. Microzonation does not change that. What it does is tell a planner **which parts of Delhi, on which deposits, will respond how** within Zone IV — which is the difference between an obligation and a decision.

### 5. The risk identity — four terms, not three

⚠️ Lesson 13 used the Core owner's three-term identity: damage = **hazard × site condition × building stock**. ✅ Khullar offers a four-term formulation that is strictly richer and that becomes the organising equation of the Indian risk arc:

```text
   SEISMIC RISK  =  HAZARD  x  EXPOSURE  x  VULNERABILITY  x  LOCATION
                       |          |             |              |
     the physical _____|          |             |              |
     potential: source,           |             |              |
     magnitude, recurrence        |             |              |
                                  |             |              |
     what is there to lose: ______|             |              |
     people, buildings, lifelines,              |              |
     economic assets                            |              |
                                                |              |
     how easily it fails: ______________________|              |
     construction type, code compliance, age,                  |
     maintenance, preparedness                                 |
                                                               |
     the site itself: _________________________________________|
     soil, water table, slope, proximity to the source,
     distance to a free face, fault proximity

   ==> IT IS A PRODUCT, NOT A SUM. If ANY term goes to zero, risk goes
       to zero. Barren Island has HAZARD and near-zero EXPOSURE (L7).
       The Indo-Gangetic plain has moderate HAZARD and very high values
       on the other THREE terms (L13).
   ==> AND THE POLICY POINT: only HAZARD is beyond human control.
       Exposure, vulnerability and location are all DECISIONS.
```

❗ **That last line is the whole governance argument of the Indian arc in one sentence**, and it is why the Advanced owner states that the advanced issue is *"converting zonation into microzonation, ductile design, retrofitting and land-use control — not merely reporting each felt tremor."* ⚠️ **Zonation is the input to three decisions, not an output to be reported.**

❗ 🔁 **Where this lesson stops.** The **drafting, approval, notification, enforcement and compliance inspection** of building codes; the **institutional architecture** of disaster administration; **retrofitting programmes** and their financing; and the **economics of construction cost** beyond the single illustrative point above — all belong to other owners and are **named once and routed**. ⚠️ What Topic 03 keeps is **why a zone exists, what it represents, at what scale it is valid, and why changing its basis changes an obligation**.

### 6. The India-applied conclusion: what a zone does and does not mean

| A seismic zone **does** | A seismic zone **does not** |
|---|---|
| Express **expected intensity and design ground motion** for a region | Predict **when** an earthquake will occur anywhere in it |
| Set a **minimum design obligation** for new construction | Guarantee that a building meeting it will survive any event |
| Generalise over a **large area at small scale** | Represent **site conditions** — that is microzonation's work |
| Encode a **decision about acceptable risk** | State a purely physical fact about the rocks |
| Change when the **assessment basis or the evidence** changes | Change because a single tremor occurred |

❗ **The last row restates the Core owner's own control**, which is binding on this file: *"no single tremor validates a deterministic recurrence or zone change."* ⚠️ That control and the 2025-26 episode point the same way from opposite directions — **a map changes when the method or the evidence base changes, through a formal instrument, and it is equally capable of changing back.**

### Comparison, objection and the residual

| | Macro-zonation (IS 1893 Part 1: 2016) | Microzonation |
|---|---|---|
| Scale | National, small scale | ✅ **Site-specific; Delhi at 1:10,000** |
| Input | Regional seismicity, source zones, attenuation | **Local soil columns, water table, topography, borehole data** |
| Output | **Four zones, II to V**, with design ground motion | ✅ **GIS-based site-specific hazard and risk products** |
| Function | A **minimum obligation** for design | **Refinement** for planning, siting and prioritisation |
| Relation | Controlling for code compliance | ❗ **Refines; does not replace, and creates no new national zone** |
| ✅ Indian status | In force; confirmed **3 March 2026** | ✅ **Delhi complete; Jabalpur and Guwahati studies; Chennai, Coimbatore, Bhubaneshwar and Mangalore in progress** |

**Strongest objection.** The 2025-26 episode shows that the science was overruled by cost. Teaching it as a lesson about method rather than about politics is evasive.

**Reply.** The episode is genuinely about both, and the honest answer says so **in that order**. ❗ The recorded objections were **not only financial**: a technical body reported that the proposed hazard values exceeded what its own **site investigations** indicated, and commentators noted that the model permitted peak ground accelerations approaching **1 g** against global practice nearer **0.4 g** — those are **methodological** objections. ⚠️ The financial and administrative objections were also real and were recorded, and in a country where roughly **80 per cent of housing is non-engineered**, the relationship between a code's stringency and its actual effect on the building stock is a legitimate question rather than an evasion. ❗ **The defensible position is the one this lesson teaches: a zone is a decision, taken under scientific uncertainty and fiscal constraint, through a formal instrument that can be reversed** — and a candidate who writes that has described a governance process accurately rather than taking a side.

⚠️ **Residual.** ❗ **The two Gazette notifications were not obtained as primary documents**, so the account above is recorded at the level of **corroborated first-order reporting**. ⚠️ Further, the technical content of the withdrawn 2025 standard is known here **only through that reporting**, so this edition asserts **no zone boundary, no design coefficient and no hazard value from it** — only the dated fact of its introduction, its stated features as reported, and its withdrawal.

### UPSC application

- **Syllabus:** "important Geophysical phenomena such as earthquakes" (Mains GS-I, direct ownership) and **"Disaster and disaster management"** (Mains GS-III) in a **support** capacity — the physical and conceptual content of zonation, not its administration.
- **Verified linkage:** no owned Mains demand sits here alone, and this is deliberate: the lesson's value is that it supplies the **governance capstone** for any GS-III disaster answer and the **precision** that keeps a GS-I answer accurate about Indian zonation. 🔁 Infrastructure-against-risk and community-participation demands are routed to the disaster-management owner.
- **Probable framing:** three sentences — the operative standard with its edition number and four zones; the macro-against-micro distinction with the Delhi scale; and the design-decision-not-prediction point, dated with the 2025-26 episode if the question invites currency.
- **Traps:** presenting **Zone VI as current**; writing "Zone I" into the current map; treating microzonation as a replacement for macro-zonation or as a creator of new national zones; reading a zone as a prediction; quoting a design coefficient or a hazard value from the withdrawn standard; describing the withdrawal as purely financial; citing the Gazette notifications as if they had been read.
- **Answer use:** *"India's operative standard is IS 1893 (Part 1): 2016, with four seismic zones from II to V and no Zone I; a zone is a design generalisation expressing expected intensity and design ground motion, not a prediction — which is why the revised 2025 standard, notified on 6 November 2025 with a new Zone VI, could be withdrawn on 3 March 2026 without any change in the underlying geology."*

Revision notes:

- ✅ ❗ **Operative: IS 1893 (Part 1): 2016 — FOUR zones, II, III, IV, V.** Confirmed in force by the **Gazette notification of 3 March 2026**.
- ✅ **Zone V** very high risk, **MSK IX and above** — Himalayan/NE belt, **Rann of Kachchh**, **Andaman and Nicobar**. ✅ **Zone IV** high, **MSK VIII** — **Delhi** and foothill/plain sectors. ✅ **Zone III** moderate, **MSK VII** — much of peninsular and coastal India. ✅ **Zone II** low, **MSK VI or lower**.
- ❗ ✅ **There is NO ZONE I on the current map.** ⚠️ An earlier scheme ran **I to V** with an **MSK legend** (very high IX / high VIII / moderate VII / low VI / very low V); ❗ **Zone I was merged upward** — the minimum design requirement in India is **not zero**. Older I-V maps are a **superseded earlier standard**; say so, do not renumber silently.
- ⚠️ ❗ **MSK and Modified Mercalli are comparable but not identical**; the Indian descriptors use **MSK**.
- 📰 **6 November 2025:** BIS Gazette notification introduced **IS 1893 (Part 1): 2025** — updated **probabilistic seismic hazard assessment** framework, **more than 60 %** of the landmass in moderate-to-high risk zones, and a **new highest-risk Zone VI** over **parts of Kashmir, the North-East and Kutch**.
- 📰 **mid-2025:** the **National Dam Safety Authority** objected — values *"much higher than what we were reading from our site investigations"*. 📰 **February 2026:** the **Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs** wrote to the **Cabinet Secretariat** on *"technical and financial concerns"* and *"materially significant financial implications"*, especially **metro rail**. 📰 ❗ **3 March 2026:** a **fresh Gazette notification withdrew** the November 2025 notification, **restoring IS 1893 (Part 1): 2016 with immediate effect**. ❗ **Zone VI was live for under four months and is NOT the current map.**
- 📰 Reported impact of the withdrawn framework: ~**45 %** no change · ~**42 %** one-zone increase, construction costs **+10-20 %** · ~**6 %** two-zone increase, costs **+over 30 %**; dam costs **+10-20 %**, up to **+50 %** in the highest zones; peak ground acceleration allowed near **1 g** against global practice nearer **0.4 g**; ~**80 %** of Indian housing is **non-engineered**.
- ❗ **Evidentiary limit:** the **Gazette notifications were not obtained as primary documents**; the account rests on **corroborated first-order reporting quoting both by date**. ❗ **No zone boundary, design coefficient or hazard value from the withdrawn standard is asserted.**
- ❗ **The teaching point:** **a seismic zone is a DESIGN DECISION, not a scientific prediction.** The geology of Kashmir, the North-East and Kutch was unchanged throughout; what changed twice was **the instrument converting hazard into obligation**.
- ⚠️ **Deterministic-leaning** — worst credible event, fewer explicit assumptions, one scenario. **Probabilistic (PSHA)** — all sources weighted by occurrence rate, a hazard curve, a **chosen** exceedance level, and **explicit source, recurrence and attenuation models**, each contestable. ❗ **The dispute was about the model, not the geology.**
- 📰 ✅ **Microzonation** = *"classifying a region into zones of relatively similar exposure to various earthquake-related effects"*, giving **GIS-based, site-specific** products. ✅ **Delhi completed at 1:10,000**; ✅ **Jabalpur and Guwahati**; ✅ **Chennai, Coimbatore, Bhubaneshwar and Mangalore in progress**.
- ❗ **Microzonation REFINES macro-zonation; it does not replace it and creates no new national zone.** ✅ Delhi stays **Zone IV**; microzonation says **which parts of Delhi respond how**.
- ✅ **Seismic Risk = Hazard × Exposure × Vulnerability × Location.** ❗ **A product, not a sum** — any zero term zeroes the risk. ❗ **Only hazard is beyond human control**; exposure, vulnerability and location are **decisions**.
- ✅ The advanced issue is *"converting zonation into microzonation, ductile design, retrofitting and land-use control — not merely reporting each felt tremor."* 🔁 Code drafting, enforcement, retrofitting finance and construction economics are **routed**.
- ❗ ✅ **No single tremor validates a deterministic recurrence or a zone change.** A map changes through a **formal instrument**, and can change **back**.

### Concept check

**Question:** In March 2026 a candidate writes: "India has six seismic zones, with Zone VI the highest, covering Kashmir, the North-East and Kutch." Identify every error, give the correct statement, and explain what the candidate's source most probably was and why relying on it was a method failure rather than a memory failure.

**Model answer:** There are two errors and one defensible kernel. The first error is the zone count: **India's operative map has four zones, II to V**, under **IS 1893 (Part 1): 2016**, with **Zone V** the highest; there is also **no Zone I**, the lowest class of an earlier five-zone scheme having been merged upward. The second error is temporal rather than factual: a Zone VI covering parts of Kashmir, the North-East and Kutch **did exist**, introduced by a Gazette notification of **6 November 2025** under IS 1893 (Part 1): 2025, which moved to an updated probabilistic hazard framework — but it was **withdrawn by a fresh Gazette notification on 3 March 2026**, restoring the 2016 standard with immediate effect. The correct statement is therefore: *"India's operative standard is IS 1893 (Part 1): 2016, with four zones from II to V; a revised standard introducing a Zone VI was notified on 6 November 2025 and withdrawn on 3 March 2026."* The candidate's source was almost certainly material written during the four months the notification was live. The failure is one of **method, not memory**: a technical standard carries an **edition number and a date**, and any statement about it is only as current as the date attached. Writing "six zones" without an edition and a date is unverifiable even when it happens to be true.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not treat a published standard as a permanent fact about the physical world. The geology did not change between November 2025 and March 2026; the instrument did, twice. A zone is an administrative decision about design obligation, and administrative decisions are reversible in a way that tectonics is not.

**Original Mains practice [10 marks]:** "Seismic zonation is a statement about design obligation, not about the Earth." Examine. *(Examine — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model (about 150 words):** The statement is largely correct and usefully provocative. A zone map is drawn from geological evidence — source zones, seismicity, attenuation — but what it issues is an **obligation**: expected intensity converted into design ground motion that new construction must meet. Three features show that the instrument, not the Earth, is being described. First, the scheme itself has changed: an earlier map ran from Zone I to Zone V before the lowest class was merged upward, leaving the four zones II to V of IS 1893 (Part 1): 2016. Second, a revised standard notified on 6 November 2025 introduced a Zone VI and was withdrawn on 3 March 2026 without any change in the underlying tectonics. Third, the same hazard supports different obligations depending on the chosen exceedance level. The qualification is that the evidence base is genuinely geological, so the decision is constrained, not arbitrary.

**Rubric (quantified, 10 marks):** 3 for design obligation identified as the output, with expected intensity converted into design ground motion; 3 for both supersessions used as evidence — the merged Zone I and the dated 2025-26 notification and withdrawal; 2 for the probabilistic exceedance-level point showing that one hazard supports several obligations; 1 for the operative standard named with its edition number; 1 for the constrained-not-arbitrary qualification. Deduct 3 for presenting Zone VI as current and 2 for placing Zone I on the current map.

Four spines and three maps. Everything below is the discipline that makes them usable under time pressure.

### 1. Directive decoding — what each word actually asks

✅ The Core owner's decoding table is reproduced and extended here, because the single commonest failure in this topic is answering a different directive from the one printed.

| ✅ If the question says | ✅ It is really asking for | ✅ Do **not** |
|---|---|---|
| *"Define mantle plume and explain its role in plate tectonics"* | Plume anatomy (head and tail), the age-progressive chain, and its use as a **motion reference frame** | Describe ordinary arc volcanoes |
| *"Explain the geophysical characteristics of the circum-Pacific belt"* | **Subduction anatomy** — trench, inclined seismic zone, arc, back-arc — and why seismicity and explosive volcanism coincide | Merely say *"the Ring of Fire has many volcanoes"* |
| *"What are tsunamis? How and where are they formed?"* | Vertical sea-floor displacement → long-wavelength wave → shoaling amplification → coastal consequence, **plus the source geography** | Treat a tsunami as a large wind wave |
| *"Assess the environmental impact of volcanic eruptions"* | The **six pathways** and the **fertility counterpoint** | Write a one-sided disaster narrative |
| *"Why is the Himalaya seismic but not volcanic?"* | **Continent-continent collision**: shortening without subduction of dense oceanic crust | Assume all mountains are volcanic |
| **Mention** (extended here) | **Light identification**, then weight on the second half of the question | Narrate each named item at length |
| **Discuss** (extended here) | A **characterised object** with a position, evidence and a qualification | Produce an undifferentiated list |

⚠️ **The standing directive deserves its own full answer because it recurs in several disguises.** *Why is the Himalaya seismic but not volcanic?* — ⚠️ **Earthquakes require strain and rupture; volcanoes require melt.** In a continent-continent collision the crust shortens and thickens, so strain accumulates abundantly on the thrust system and is released seismically; but **no dense oceanic slab descends to dehydrate**, so there is **no flux melting**, and the belt generates no arc magma. ✅ Leong's observation — *"The Himalayas have, surprisingly, no active volcano at all"* — supplies the fact; the melting routes of Lesson 1 supply the reason. ❗ **Two sentences, and the question is finished.**

### 2. The reusable spines, with their weighting

⚠️ The four spines in the visual are the owned demands. ✅ The Core owner also supplies a **15-mark seismic-hazard spine** which is the general-purpose structure for any India-risk question:

- ⚠️ **(i) The tectonic source** — collision belt against subduction belt against intra-plate.
- ⚠️ **(ii) The ground-motion modifier** — depth, soil amplification on alluvium, liquefaction on saturated sediment.
- ⚠️ **(iii) The human modifier** — building stock, density, code compliance, preparedness.
- ❗ **(iv) The qualification** — **prediction of time is not currently possible**, so **zoning, codes and retrofitting, not forecasting, carry the policy weight**.
- ⚠️ **(v) A conclusion that grades where risk is concentrated and why.**

❗ ✅ The owner attaches the boundary instruction to the spine itself: *"Institutional disaster-management doctrine belongs to the Disaster-Management folder — cross-link, do not duplicate."* ⚠️ In practice this means **one routing sentence** inside the answer, which costs five words and demonstrates command of the syllabus rather than ignorance of the adjacent paper.

⚠️ **Structure the whole hazard answer on the four-term identity of Lesson 15** — **Hazard × Exposure × Vulnerability × Location** — rather than on a list of earthquakes. ❗ It is a product, so a single near-zero term dominates; and only the first term is beyond human control.

### 3. Two reusable evidence units

✅ The Core owner supplies two fully structured evidence units, each in the form **claim → evidence → significance → limitation**, and both are directly transferable into a Mains paragraph.

> ✅ **Claim:** the same tectonic setting produces both catastrophic risk and exceptional productivity. **Evidence:** volcanic regions such as **Java, Sicily and India's Deccan** carry dense populations on ash- and basalt-derived soils. **Significance:** it explains why hazard exposure persists despite known risk — people are not being irrational, they are **trading episodic hazard for continuous fertility**. **Limitation:** the trade-off holds only where **warning, evacuation and land-use control** exist; without them the same fertility becomes a mortality multiplier.

> ✅ **Claim:** the interior of the Earth is known by inference, not observation. **Evidence:** S-waves fail to cross the outer core, generating a shadow zone. **Significance:** it establishes the liquid outer core. **Limitation:** wave evidence constrains **state and boundaries**, not detailed composition. ❗ 🔁 In this topic that unit is used **only** to demonstrate the inference-from-evidence structure; the internal-structure argument itself belongs to the crust-and-rocks topic.

⚠️ **A third unit, built in this edition and specific to the India arc:**

> ⚠️ **Claim:** India's most hazardous region is not its most at-risk region. **Evidence:** the Himalayan belt records the largest and most frequent events, yet the **Indo-Gangetic basin**, recording mostly magnitude 6 to 6.5, combines **thick amplifying alluvium**, the country's **highest population density** and a predominantly **non-engineered** building stock. **Significance:** risk is a product of four terms and only one of them is the earthquake. **Limitation:** no amplification factor, population figure or loss estimate is asserted, and the comparison is structural rather than quantified.

### 4. Diagram discipline in the examination hall

⚠️ A diagram in a Mains answer is worth drawing **only if it does work that the prose would otherwise have to do**. Four rules make that test operational.

- ❗ **One diagram per ten-mark answer; at most two per twenty-mark answer.** More than that is time taken from content.
- ❗ **Label it, and make at least two labels non-obvious.** A section labelled only "trench" and "volcano" has added nothing; the same section labelled with the **inclined seismic zone**, the **mantle wedge** and the **back-arc basin** has added three facts.
- ❗ **Draw the diagram that the directive needs**, not the one that is easiest. For a plume question, the age-progressive chain **with its age arrow** is the diagram; a cross-section of a volcano is not.
- ❗ **Refer to the diagram in the text at least once.** An unreferenced diagram reads as decoration; *"as the section shows, focal depth increases away from the trench"* converts it into evidence.

⚠️ **The four highest-value diagrams in this topic**, each earning its space: the **Wadati-Benioff section** for any belt question; the **hot-spot chain with an age arrow** for the plume question; the **shoaling profile** for the tsunami question; and the **site-response stack** for any damage or vulnerability question.

### 5. The consolidated trap register

| # | Trap | The correction |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | The mantle is molten | Hot but **solid**; pressure raises the melting point. Melt needs **decompression, flux or heat transfer** |
| 2 | Shield volcanoes are steep | **Gentle** — fluid basic lava. Acid lava builds the steep cones |
| 3 | A caldera is a big crater | **Crater is constructional; caldera is destructional** — collapse after magma withdrawal |
| 4 | All volcanoes pass through active → dormant → extinct | **Evidence-based activity classes, not a one-way life cycle**; absence of a historic eruption is insufficient |
| 5 | The Deccan once had great cones | **Fissure eruption** builds plateaux; no cones were ever required |
| 6 | Lonar is a volcanic crater | ❗ It is an **impact crater** in Deccan basalt. The deposit decides, not the outline |
| 7 | A line of volcanoes implies a plume | ❗ The diagnostic is the **age gradient**, not the alignment |
| 8 | Plumes drive plate motion | They do **not**. They are a **vertical** process superimposed on a **horizontal** one |
| 9 | The Himalaya is volcanic | **Collision belt** — strain but no slab, hence no melt. ✅ *"no active volcano at all"* |
| 10 | "Richter" is the current scale | **Local magnitude saturates**; great events are reported in **moment magnitude (Mw)** |
| 11 | One magnitude unit doubles the energy | **×10 amplitude, ≈ ×31.6 energy** (energy ∝ amplitude^1.5) |
| 12 | Magnitude and intensity are the same | **One magnitude, many intensities**; intensity depends on distance, depth, **soil and construction** |
| 13 | Focus and epicentre are the same point | Focus is **inside**; epicentre is **vertically above it** at the surface |
| 14 | One station can locate an earthquake | A single station gives **distance, not direction** — **three or more** are required |
| 15 | A tsunami is a tidal wave | **Seismic sea wave**; tides are gravitational and irrelevant |
| 16 | Any undersea earthquake makes a tsunami | **Vertical** displacement is required; **horizontal motion usually generates none** |
| 17 | The first wave is the biggest | It is a **train**; the first is often not the largest |
| 18 | Drawback always precedes a tsunami | ❗ It does **not**; respond to shaking and official warning |
| 19 | "Below eight metres, evacuate" | ❗ A **dated simplification**; run-up varies with bathymetry and coastal form |
| 20 | Alluvial plains are safest in an earthquake | **Thick alluvium amplifies shaking and can liquefy** |
| 21 | The Peninsula is aseismic | **Relatively stable, not immune** — **Koyna 1967, Latur 1993, Jabalpur 1997** |
| 22 | India's highest zone is Zone I | **Zones II to V; Zone V is highest; there is no Zone I** |
| 23 | India now has a Zone VI | ❗ **Notified 6 November 2025, withdrawn 3 March 2026.** **IS 1893 (Part 1): 2016 is operative** |
| 24 | Microzonation replaces macro-zonation | It **refines** it at site scale and **creates no new national zone** |
| 25 | A zone predicts an earthquake | A zone is a **design generalisation**, never a prediction; and **no single tremor changes a map** |
| 26 | A return period means an event is due | **Observed average over the catalogue**, not a forecast; occurrence is **clustered, not periodic** |
| 27 | Prediction and early warning are the same | **Prediction is unavailable**; **early warning** detects an event already under way |

### 6. What not to write, and one lesson in source criticism

❗ **Six things that cost marks in this topic and should never appear.**

- ❗ **A casualty, cost, area or explosivity figure quoted from memory.** Every number in a geophysics answer should carry a source, an era or a hedge.
- ❗ **Disaster-management doctrine in a GS-I answer.** 🔁 Response, relief, institutional architecture and preparedness programmes are another paper's; **one routing clause** is the correct treatment.
- ❗ **Cyclones.** They are named in the same syllabus clause and are owned elsewhere. **Name the clause, route the cyclone.**
- ❗ **A deterministic sentence about the future.** *"A great earthquake is due in the central Himalaya"* is not a geographical statement; it is a prediction, and prediction is unavailable.
- ❗ **An answer-key letter, truth marking or elimination cue for an objective question.** Teach the mechanism the question tests and leave the key alone.
- ❗ **An undated percentage presented as a universal constant** — volcano counts, earthquake counts and belt shares all drift with the detection network.

⚠️ **And one worked example of source criticism, because it is the most transferable skill in this topic.** One of the local sources tabulates a *"1737 Oct. 11, Kolkata, India, 300,000 deaths"* earthquake. ❗ **That entry is not used anywhere in this edition**, because that entry is a long-standing misattribution — the catastrophe of that date was a **cyclone**, not an earthquake — and the casualty figure has been repeatedly challenged. ⚠️ The transferable lesson is not the fact; it is the **habit**: when a tabulated entry in an otherwise reliable book is **internally inconsistent with the book's own running text**, or is wildly out of line with everything around it, the correct response is to **exclude it and say why**, not to reproduce it because it is printed. ❗ The same table dates the 2004 Banda Aceh event to January while the book's own tsunami text correctly gives **26 December 2004** — which is the internal inconsistency that condemns the table as a whole.

### 7. The assembled India-hook inventory — a ready example bank

⚠️ Fifteen lessons have each deposited one named Indian example. Assembled, they are an example bank that covers every dimension this topic can be examined on, and **no Mains answer in this topic should lack at least one of them**.

| Lesson | 🇮🇳 India hook | Use it when the question concerns |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | **The Deccan as a plateau, not a cone**, and the lava chemistry that made it so | Lava type, landform control, magma viscosity |
| 2 | **Deccan dyke swarms** as feeders; the **Malani igneous suite** as a batholithic analogue | Intrusive forms; eruption products; one magmatic system |
| 3 | **Barren Island's arc-style activity** in the Andaman arc | Eruption style; Indian volcanism; arc settings |
| 4 | **Deccan Traps as a fissure landform**; **Lonar as an impact crater** | Landform classification; crater against caldera; evidence discipline |
| 5 | **Réunion plume and the Deccan; Kerguelen plume and the Rajmahal Traps** | Plumes; flood basalts; large igneous provinces |
| 6 | The **Andaman-Sunda arc**; **Barren and Narcondam within 80 km east of the Andamans** | Belt architecture; India's own segment of the Ring of Fire |
| 7 | **Barren Island: full hazard, near-zero exposure** | Hazard against disaster; the risk identity |
| 8 | **Koyna 1967 and the Shivaji Sagar reservoir impounded in 1962** | Causes of earthquakes; anthropogenic seismicity; trigger against cause |
| 9 | **Central Seismological Observatory, Shillong, 1902**, and the national network it anchors | Wave reading; location; institutional capability |
| 10 | The **national reporting convention** and the paired 2025 Indian events | Magnitude against intensity; why both are needed |
| 11 | The **Sunda Trench and its Andaman segment** — India as both far-field and near-field | Tsunami generation; source geography; warning time |
| 12 | **Indira Point almost washed out; Nagapattinam's 9,500 hectares** | Tsunami consequence; geomorphic agency; salinisation |
| 13 | The **Indo-Gangetic alluvium** as an amplification problem | Site effects; liquefaction; hazard against risk |
| 14 | The **Everest-Badrinath "almost stable" stretch** against the **Kangra-Dalhousie-Gulmarg arc** | Segmentation of the Himalayan front; regional gradient |
| 15 | The **6 November 2025 notification and its 3 March 2026 withdrawal** | Zonation as a decision; deterministic against probabilistic; source criticism |

### Comparison, objection and the residual

| | A ten-mark answer | A fifteen-mark answer | A twenty-mark answer |
|---|---|---|---|
| Word budget | ~150 | ~250 | ~250 |
| Structure | One spine, executed completely | Spine **plus** a comparative paragraph | Spine, comparison, **and an explicit counter-position** |
| Diagrams | **One**, if it does work | One, possibly two | At most two |
| Named examples | **Two or three**, one Indian | Four, at least two Indian | Five, with at least one dated |
| Qualification | One sentence | One sentence plus a limitation on the evidence | **Two**, including the limits of the evidence itself |
| Fatal omission | Skipping the mechanism | Skipping the comparison | Skipping the counter-position |

**Strongest objection.** Answer craft is padding. The marks are in the content, and time spent on spines is time not spent learning facts.

**Reply.** The four owned demands in this topic carry **four different directive words**, and the single most common way to lose marks here is **misallocation**, not ignorance. ⚠️ A candidate who writes a full narrative of each 2021 eruption has answered *"describe"* when the paper said *"mention"*, and has spent the marks allocated to **regional environmental impact** on identification. ❗ A candidate who lists volcanoes in the Ring of Fire has answered *"enumerate"* when the paper said *"discuss the geophysical characteristics"*. ⚠️ **Both failures occur in candidates who know the content perfectly well.** The spines exist to prevent exactly that, and they take no additional facts to learn — they are the facts of Lessons 1 to 15, pre-ordered.

⚠️ **Residual.** Directive conventions are **not formally defined by the examiner**, so any decoding table is an informed reading of past papers rather than an authority. ❗ The spines should therefore be used as **defaults that are adapted to the exact wording printed**, never as templates applied regardless of it.

### UPSC application

- **Syllabus:** all three directly owned clauses — "Salient features of world's physical geography", the geophysical-phenomena clause for **earthquakes, Tsunami and Volcanic activity**, and the Prelims physical-geography clause — plus the GS-III disaster clause in a support capacity.
- **Verified linkage:** **consolidates all four owned Mains demands — 2018, 2020, 2021 and 2025 — and all four owned objective demands.** ❗ *Answer-neutral throughout: year, paper, number and conceptual demand only.*
- **Probable framing:** this lesson is the revision instrument. In the week before the paper, the three maps and the four spines are the whole of this topic's active recall load; Lessons 1 to 15 supply the evidence that fills them.
- **Traps:** every entry in §5, and above all the three that an examiner can check instantly — **Zone VI as current**, **Zone I on the current map**, and a **deterministic sentence about a future earthquake**.
- **Answer use:** begin with the directive, not with the subject. Read the printed verb, select the spine, allocate the words to the heavier half of the question, and write the qualification before running out of time rather than after.

Revision notes:

- **Four spines, four directives:** *Define and explain* (plume) · *Discuss* (Circum-Pacific) · *Mention* (eruptions and impact) · *Explain* (tsunami formation and consequences).
- ❗ **"Mention" is light; the marks are in the second half of the question.** ❗ **"Discuss" wants a characterised object**, not a list.
- ✅ **Standing directive:** *Why is the Himalaya seismic but not volcanic?* → **strain without a slab**: collision shortens and thickens crust, but **no descending oceanic slab, hence no flux melting**. ✅ *"no active volcano at all."*
- ✅ **15-mark hazard spine:** tectonic source → ground-motion modifier → human modifier → **prediction is unavailable, so zoning, codes and retrofitting carry the policy weight** → graded conclusion. ✅ **Cross-link disaster management; do not duplicate it.**
- ⚠️ Structure India-risk answers on **Hazard × Exposure × Vulnerability × Location** — a **product**; **only hazard is beyond human control**.
- ✅ **Evidence unit 1:** volcanic regions are hazardous **and** densely farmed — **Java, Sicily, the Deccan**; limitation: needs **warning and land-use control**. ✅ **Evidence unit 2:** inference from wave evidence, with its own limit. ⚠️ **Unit 3 (this edition):** India's most hazardous region is not its most at-risk region.
- **Diagram discipline:** **one per 10-marker**, at most two per 20-marker; **label two non-obvious things**; draw what the **directive** needs; **refer to it in the text**.
- **Four highest-value diagrams:** **Wadati-Benioff section** · **hot-spot chain with an age arrow** · **shoaling profile** · **site-response stack**.
- **Three maps:** **world belts** (Circum-Pacific, Mediterranean-Alpine to the Himalaya marked seismic only, Atlantic ridge with Iceland and the Azores, dormant Atlantic margins, East African Rift, **Mt Cameroon**, West Indies, **Hawaii with an age arrow**, geothermal trio) · **India regions and zones** (seven regions; **II-V with no Zone I**; **Koyna, Latur, Jabalpur, Bhuj**; the **Everest-Badrinath** segmentation; the **Bettiah-Purnea** belt) · **the Andaman arc and the 2004 source** (**Sunda Trench**, fore-arc ridge, **Barren and Narcondam**, back-arc sea, **3.5° N 95° E tri-junction**, ~1,000 km rupture, **Indira Point**, **Nagapattinam**, **Kalpakkam**, **ITEWC at Hyderabad — inland**).
- **Twenty-seven-entry trap register** — the three most checkable being **Zone VI as current**, **Zone I on the current map**, and a **prediction sentence**.
- ❗ **Never write:** a memory-quoted casualty, cost, area or index figure · disaster-management doctrine · cyclones · a deterministic future claim · an objective answer key or elimination cue · an undated percentage as a constant.
- ❗ **Source criticism, worked:** a tabulated historic-earthquake entry is **excluded** — the event of that date was a **cyclone**, and the same table misdates **Banda Aceh** to January while the book's own text correctly gives **26 December 2004**. ⚠️ **Internal inconsistency condemns the table, not just the row.**
- ⚠️ **Fifteen India hooks** are assembled as a ready example bank; ❗ **no Mains answer in this topic should lack at least one.**
- ⚠️ **Residual:** directive conventions are **not formally defined**; adapt the spine to the printed wording.

### Concept check

**Question:** A paper carries the instruction: "Mention the major volcanic eruptions of a recent year and discuss their regional environmental impact." A candidate writes 150 words, of which 110 narrate three eruptions in detail and 40 describe ash damage. Diagnose every failure and state the allocation that should have been used.

**Model answer:** Four failures, three of them structural. **First, directive confusion**: the paper carries two directives of different weight — *mention*, which asks only for identification, and *discuss*, which asks for a developed treatment — and the candidate has inverted the weighting, spending nearly three-quarters of the answer on the light half. **Second, misread object**: *"regional environmental impact"* is the substance of the question, and forty words cannot carry six impact pathways, a regional differentiation and a conclusion. **Third, a one-sided impact account**: environmental impact includes soil formation, geothermal resource and mineral concentration as well as ash, gas and pyroclastic currents, and omitting the benefit column forfeits the balance mark and leaves the answer unable to explain why volcanic regions are densely settled. **Fourth, probable number abuse**: detailed narration of three eruptions in 110 words almost certainly carries casualty or area figures that cannot be sourced. The allocation should have been roughly **35 words to mention** — name, place and date for each eruption, no narration — and **115 words to impact**, structured as pathways, then a regional differentiation noting that effusive events destroy property slowly while explosive arc events kill quickly, then the episodic-hazard-against-continuous-return balance, closing on the qualification that the trade-off requires warning and land-use control.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not treat the first directive in a compound question as the main one. The weighting follows the **substance of what is asked**, not the order in which the verbs appear, and in this topic the heavier half is almost always the impact, consequence or characterisation clause rather than the identification clause.

**Original Mains practice [10 marks]:** "In the geophysical phenomena of General Studies Paper I, the mechanism is the answer and the example is the evidence." Examine, with reference to any two owned demands. *(Examine — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model (about 150 words):** The proposition holds, and two demands demonstrate it. On mantle plumes, the mechanism is that a near-stationary deep upwelling feeds a long-lived hot spot while the plate above moves, so the chain it builds becomes age-progressive and therefore records the direction and relative rate of plate motion. Hawaii, the Deccan with the Réunion plume and the Rajmahal Traps with the Kerguelen plume are evidence for that mechanism; recited without it they are merely a list. On tsunamis, the mechanism is the vertical displacement of the entire water column, with the corollary that horizontal sea-floor motion usually generates nothing; the 2004 Sumatra event, with about fifteen metres of vertical slip along a thousand-kilometre rupture, is evidence that the mechanism operated. The qualification is that examples are not decorative: without a named, dated instance a mechanism reads as assertion. Mechanism and example are therefore complementary, with the mechanism carrying the analytical marks.

**Rubric (quantified, 10 marks):** 3 for the plume mechanism stated with the age-progression consequence; 3 for the tsunami mechanism stated with the horizontal-motion corollary; 2 for named examples attached to each as evidence rather than as substitutes; 1 for the reply that examples remain necessary; 1 for a directive-sensitive framing. Deduct 2 for listing examples without mechanisms and 2 for any unsourced quantitative claim.

# VERIFIED PYQ LINKAGE AND ANSWER APPROACHES

## What this topic owns, and what it does not

This topic directly owns **eight verified demands** — **four Mains** and **four objective**. ❗ The four Mains demands are the **heaviest Mains load of any Geography topic audited in this lane**, and they sit on four different directive words and four unrelated objects: a mantle feature, a named tectonic belt, a dated eruption year, and a two-part process.

❗ **No 2026 General Studies Paper I demand reaches this topic.** Every one of the twenty routed 2026 GS-I demands was checked item by item; none lands here. The 2026 Essay, GS-II and GS-III ledgers and all three Civil Services Aptitude Test ledgers likewise carry **no route to this topic**. The single 2026 presence is one **Prelims** route, on a named volcano and its geopark designation, and its key is **provisional**.

❗ **No answer letter is recorded or inferred for any objective question below.** That includes the 2024 demand whose official Set-A key is physically present in the repository, and the 2026 demand whose Set-A key is held locally but provisional. **No truth marking, elimination cue or corrective option mapping appears anywhere in this file.** ❗ **No solved previous-year model answer appears**, and no replacement solved-practice workbook has been created.

## Approach notes — demand by demand

**P1 · 2018 GS-I Q6 · Define and explain · 10 marks · 150 words.** A two-part directive with roughly equal weight. *Define* in one sentence that separates **head** from **tail**: a narrow, buoyant column of anomalously hot mantle rising largely independently of plate motion, with a broad head that produces a flood-basalt province and a narrower tail that feeds a long-lived hot spot. *Explain the role* in three moves: the **age-progressive chain** as a record of plate motion, giving a **reference frame independent of magnetic-stripe evidence**; a **bend** in the chain as a record of a change in direction; and large igneous provinces as markers of **continental break-up**. Attach Hawaii, the Deccan with the Réunion plume and the Rajmahal Traps with the Kerguelen plume. ❗ Deny the false role explicitly — **plumes do not drive plate motion** — and close on the contested status. ❗ Do not describe ordinary arc volcanoes, and quote **no plume depth, age-progression rate or eruption volume**.

**P2 · 2020 GS-I Q4 · Discuss · 10 marks · 150 words.** *Discuss* at ten marks wants a **characterised object**. Open by refusing the count: characteristics are architecture. Then the architecture in order — **trench, accretionary fore-arc, volcanic arc, back-arc basin**, with the **Wadati-Benioff inclined seismic zone** as the direct geophysical image of the slab and the systematic deepening of foci away from the trench as its evidence. Then the **four outputs of one architecture**: volcanic via flux melting, seismic across three focal-depth classes, tsunamigenic via shallow megathrust slip beneath deep water, and metallogenic. One comparative clause — a ridge is effusive and shallow-only; a collision belt is seismic but **not** volcanic. One Indian clause — the **Andaman-Sunda segment**. ❗ Close on the qualification that the belt is a **ring of convergent margins of several kinds** whose unity is **mechanistic, not geometric**, and attach an era to any percentage.

**P3 · 2021 GS-I Q7 · Mention · 10 marks · 150 words.** ❗ The weighting trap is the whole question. *Mention* is light; **regional environmental impact** is the substance. Allocate roughly **35 words** to naming, placing and dating the eruptions — the 2021 set verified for this edition is **Fagradalsfjall (Iceland, from 19 March)**, **La Soufrière (St Vincent, from 9 April)**, **Nyiragongo (near Goma, 22 May)**, **Taal (Philippines, July)**, **Cumbre Vieja (La Palma, 19 September to 13 December)** and **Semeru (Java, 4 December)** — and roughly **115 words** to impact. Structure the impact as **pathways** (aerosol veil, ash fall, pyroclastic density currents, delayed lahars, gases, long-term fertility), then the analytical move that the set **spans every tectonic setting** so that the impact profile **tracks the setting**, then the **benefit column**, then the **episodic-against-continuous balance** with its warning-and-land-use qualification. ❗ Quote **no casualty, evacuation, area, plume-height or explosivity figure**.

**P4 · 2025 GS-I Q7 · Explain · 10 marks · 150 words.** Three clauses, so three blocks. **What:** a train of seismic sea waves — not tidal, not wind-driven, and not a single wave. **How and where:** ❗ the mechanism is **vertical displacement of the whole water column**, under the conditions of focal depth below 50 km, magnitude above 7.5 and a rupture of a few kilometres to over a thousand, **with the corollary that horizontal sea-floor motion usually generates none**; four triggers; and the source geography of **oceanic trenches**, with the **Sunda Trench** as the Indian Ocean's principal source and its **Andaman segment** making India both a far-field and a near-field target. **Consequences:** write them as **dimensions, not a casualty list** — human, **physiographic** (Indira Point, Trinket, Car Nicobar), **pedological** (Nagapattinam), **ecological** (reefs), **infrastructural** (Kalpakkam) — and close on the asymmetry that the wave lasted minutes while the salinity lasted years. ❗ Qualify that all figures are **reported and revised estimates**, and 🔁 route warning governance in a clause rather than developing it.

**P5 · 2018 Prelims Q85.** Mechanism owed: **Barren Island's tectonic setting**. ✅ It is a volcanic island of the **Andaman arc**, in the Bay of Bengal, and ✅ *"two volcanic islands (Barren and Narcondam) situated within 80 km east of the Andaman Islands"*; ✅ the region *"falls in a major earthquake zone"*; and the arc is a subduction system with a trench to the west and a back-arc sea behind. ❗ **No eruption date, frequency, column height or eruption list is asserted anywhere in this file.** *Answer-neutral.*

**P6 · 2023 Prelims Q65.** Mechanism owed: the **five contrasts** of Lesson 9 — compressional against shear; faster against slower; **solids-and-liquids against solids-only**; particle motion along the ray path against perpendicular to it; **body against surface**, with surface waves arriving last and causing most structural damage. ⚠️ Every statement-level distinction such a question can test is a permutation of those five. *Answer-neutral; no key held locally.*

**P7 · 2024 Prelims Q3.** Mechanism owed: the **four-class product inventory** — **lava**, **pyroclasts/tephra** (dust, ash, lapilli, scoria, pumice, bombs), **gases** (CO2, sulphur species, nitrogen, chlorine) and **secondary or mixed products** (lahars, pyroclastic density currents, stratospheric dust) — with the classes distinguished by **state** and by **mode of emplacement**. ⚠️ The half candidates omit is that **most magma never erupts**, so the intrusive family is part of the magmatic system's output. ❗ *Answer-neutral: the repository holds the official Set-A key and it is deliberately neither recorded nor inferred.*

**P8 · 2026 Prelims Q31.** 🔁 **Routed.** Geopark designation is an **institutional and conservation instrument**, owned by the environment-and-ecology folder. ⚠️ Topic 03 supplies only the physical half: what a volcanic landscape is, and why it carries **geoheritage** value — the benefit-column row in Lesson 7's ledger. ❗ *Answer-neutral; the locally held 2026 Set-A key is provisional and was not consulted.*
